commit** — if you added a workaround or noticed something off, log it here.

| #5  | 🟡 | Med    | No PWA (installable/offline). **Installable: built (2026-10-22).** `public/manifest.webmanifest` (`/play/`, `display: fullscreen`, Ember's egg at 192 / 512 px and a maskable 512) and the iOS home-screen tags in `index.html`: Add to Home Screen opens the game with no browser bars. On iOS the home-screen app has its own storage, so a family signs in once more inside it (saves come from the server). **Offline: still open, on purpose.** No service worker: Chrome no longer needs one to install; questions, saves and sign-in are online, so offline play needs an offline question bank and a save queue first; and a cached old version brings back the stale-tab trouble the save version guard handles (#101i) — an update prompt would have to come with it. `vite-plugin-pwa` (removed as unused in #87) is the tool when this is picked up. **Review round 1 (2026-10-22, `/saas-code-review` nothing found + `/saas-ux-review` 3 medium), all fixed:** a white page showed between the dark splash and the first screen while the game's script loaded — the page is the splash's colour while `#root` is empty; the grown-ups FAQ now says how to add it (and that iPhones and iPads ask to sign in once more); **open:** whether Android's keyboard covers a field in `display: fullscreen` (the window may not resize there) can't be tried without a phone — TC-2114 checks it, and `standalone` is the fallback if it does. Not yet tried on a real phone or tablet (TC-2114). |

| #191 | 🟢 | Med    | **Fixed (2026-10-24, PR #92 review round 1, UX): two players on one screen can always see whose turn it is, and the sibling's PIN pad fits any screen.** Fresh `/saas-ux-review` in the real app at 320×568, 375×667, 740×360 and 1024×768 (3 high, 3 medium, 5 low; nothing scrolled sideways). **High:** (1) the sibling's PIN pad had its 0 key, the wrong-PIN words, the lock countdown and "← That's not me" below the fold at 320×568 and 740×360, and in co-op it opened inside the 📜 Menu's own scroll box, hidden at every size — now a full-screen layer of its own (`ModalLayer` portalled to `body`), Escape and "That's not me" close it, focus back on the tile tapped. (2) On a sideways phone the duel's scoreboard and "<name>'s turn" sat above the screen all duel — each turn now scrolls the card's top into view, and the question wears a "🦊 Mia's question" chip in the player's colour. (3) The duel's top bar "Switch" (under 400 px) read as "switch turns" and two taps threw the duel away with no XP — no Switch player mid-duel ("End duel" is the way out; `ScreenTopBar`'s `switchable`). **Medium:** (4) the co-op win's team line and Player 2's XP were under the fold, and the visible XP (the kid playing's) wasn't named — they now come right under the stats, which say "🐼 Ada: …". (5) Focus fell to the page after a duel answer (`disabled` options, Next the fifth Tab) and after each 👥 Play together step — Next takes the focus and options use `aria-disabled` (#159); the panel hands the keyboard on within itself. (6) The co-op menus, where the phone changes hands, said whose move it was only in a small grey header — they wear the questions' sky / amber pill, and a live region says "Over to Mia." **Low:** a guest pace word broke mid-word at 320 ("Harde / r": the emoji now sits above it, with a little more room under 360 px); a co-op loss pushed its way out past 568 px (the 💡 tip hides on a short screen in co-op); the duel's two "Back to the Training Grounds" were 20 and 32 px tall (`min-h-11`); the kid playing's level badge sat beside Player 2's XP (no badges on the duel's screens, `badges`); "Cheer Valor on" used the hero's name (now the kid's: "Cheer Ada on"); 👥 Play together sat 1,450 px down a phone's menu (now under Ember's card). Seen, not ours: at 740×360 a solo battle's commands already sit below the fold (co-op's chip adds about 20 px; a co-op loss's "To Fen's Hollow" sits 26 px below it there too — the page scrolls to it), and the duel's topic tiles carry TopicSelect's white-on-light text. |
| #190 | 🔴 | Med    | **Open, the owner's call (PR #92 review round 1, finding 2): a sibling's own open game can write over XP given to them here.** `giveKidXp` never writes over a write it didn't read, but the other direction isn't guarded: Mia's game left open on her tablet (or another tab) keeps her profile in memory, and its next XP write is absolute (`profileStore.addXp` → `{xp: stale + n, level, level_xp}`), so a gift made on Ada's device meanwhile is erased from the row; this device's copy of Mia (moved by the gift) later merges by "the higher wins", so one side's XP is lost. The #151(a) class (no compare-and-swap on the profile's own writes), made routine by two-player play. Fix: guard `addXp`'s update on the XP last read (on a miss, read the row and add on top, as `giveKidXp` does), or re-read the profile when the tab comes back into view; or accept it. |
| #189 | 🟢 | Med    | **Fixed (2026-10-24, PR #92 review round 1, after its merge): a sibling's XP is never lost, and their day goes up with it.** Fresh `/saas-code-review` (3 medium, 2 low); finding 2 is #190. (1) The sibling's day reached the database as "played, 0 XP": `notePlayDay` sent it at once, then the gift's XP waited behind the 2-minute wait between sends, and nothing sends another kid's day from this device, so it waited until they loaded here — or was dropped after 7 days. Now the day goes up once the gift is saved, with its XP, at once (`addDayXp`, then `flushPlayDays(kid, { now: true })`); a failed gift still notes the day played. Proved with the real day records (`kidXp.playDays.test` fails on the old code). (3) Owed XP left storage before the write, which had no timeout: a hung connection showed "Saving…" for ever, a closed tab lost the gift, and a lost answer to a write that landed paid it twice. Now the gift is kept as *sending* while it's written (`hazel-owed-xp-sending-<kid>`; a profile load pays it only once `SENDING_STALE_MS` old, so never one still being written), each read and update gives up after `WRITE_TIMEOUT_MS` (10 s), and an update whose answer was lost is read back before it's kept owed. (4) Owed XP is forgotten with the kid or the account (`forgetOwedXp`). (5) A save that couldn't be read said the sibling's world wasn't open: now `worldOpen: null` ("Mia's XP couldn't be counted this time."; no save at all is still a closed world). Also: `BattleArena.coop.test`'s round test was flaky — a critter's second turn may wind up a power move (1 in 5), which has no blow to block; the roll is pinned. Seen, not ours: four of `QuizRound.test`'s timing tests (#163, #173) time out when this 4-core container runs the whole suite under extra load (a browser running beside it); they pass on their own and on CI. |
| #188 | 🟢 | Med    | **Built (2026-10-22, the owner's calls on #186 / #187): a sibling types their PIN to join, earns XP as themselves, and faces a boss's power moves too.** (1) **PIN:** tapping a family kid in `PlayerTwoPicker` (duel and 👥 Play together) shows their own `PinPad` ("Hi, Mia! Type your PIN.", the database's limits and locks as on "Who's playing?"); the screen's topics / "Play together!" wait meanwhile (`onAsking`); "← That's not me" goes back, and a check that answers after it picks nobody; a kid already picked this session, or one with no PIN yet, isn't asked. (2) **XP:** every family player earns XP for their right answers on bank questions (a starter pays none, #153) at their own rate (`seatXpPerRight`: 10 + their Scholar bonus), once their world is open (#122) — in a duel both players (the kid playing through `addXp`, a duel being a day played even with none); in a co-op battle Player 2's right answers plus, won, the win's XP as the kid playing gets it. A sibling's goes straight to their row (`lib/kidXp.ts` `giveKidXp`: read, then an update guarded on the XP read — a write from their own device in between means read again, up to `GUARD_TRIES`; their streak and play day follow; this device's copy of their profile moves too). The results say "Saving…", "Saved to Mia's game." or "⭐ Mia reached level 4!"; "XP starts for Mia once Mia opens the world." for a closed world; nothing for a guest. Question levels and the Library still never move for Player 2. (3) **Boss power moves:** plain blows and power moves are counted apart for who blocks (`blows.attack` / `blows.power` → `defenderFor`), so a boss's power moves come at both players in turn. **Shortcuts, to revisit:** (a) XP that can't be written (offline, a read that fails, a row that keeps changing) is owed on *this device* (`hazel-owed-xp-<kid>`): paid when that kid next loads here (`takeOwedXp` in `profileStore.loadProfile`) or with the next XP given them here — a sibling who only ever plays on their own device waits for that; the results say "It'll be added the next time Mia plays on this device." (b) A sibling's level-up shows no celebration on this screen: their power-up choice waits for them (`LevelUpModal` derives it from their level when they next play). (c) Whether their world is open is read once, as they're picked; opened mid-session elsewhere, it counts from the next pick. (d) Player 1's XP in a duel is new too (it was "just for fun"): it follows the same rules as a quiz round's, so a duel pays the same per answer as practice. (e) Counted apart, blocks are fair by kind, not strictly in turn: on a boss's rhythm one player can block two blows in a row (the test's boss: Ada, Mia, then Ada's power blow and Ada's next plain one, then Mia's power blow). **Merged with main's 📈 Progress history (#169–#184):** a sibling's saved XP goes into their day's record (`addDayXp`), owed XP is paid through `addXp` (after the load's own write, the same kid still loaded), and the answers tally (#173) is the kid playing's only — `QuestionCard`'s `noted` is off for Player 2's battle questions, and the kid playing's duel answers now count in theirs. (f) Player 2's own answers aren't tallied for *their* 📈 Progress (their XP and day are) — adding them would mean noting answers per kid from one screen. |
| #187 | 🟢 | Med    | **Built (2026-10-22): 👥 co-op battles — Player 2 plays the companion.** 📜 Menu → 👥 Play together; each round the kid playing's command, then Player 2's companion move (strike or Pair Attack on their own question, or a 📣 cheer while Ember is an egg), then the enemy's blow, blocked in turn. See CLAUDE.md → Two players. **Shortcuts, to revisit:** (a) as #186: nothing of Player 2's is saved, and the win's XP, coins and drops are the kid playing's alone — **XP resolved in #188** (a sibling earns their own; coins and drops stay the kid playing's). (b) A win can hatch Ember — the first one always does — so in a new game Player 2 cheers until then; only the companion's turn is theirs (no Guard, Items or spells of their own). (c) Pair Attacks need Player 2's super-hard pool as well as the hero's ("Getting ready…" until both are in); a co-op Pair Attack is two questions where alone it is one. (d) Co-op is per session and per device: a Switch player or a reload turns it off. (e) In the world Player 2 has no part — the kid playing walks; a "👥" mark in the world's top bar would say co-op is on before a fight starts. (f) Blocks alternate by blow, so on a boss's rhythm (attack, attack, charge, power, attack, charge, power…) its power blows keep landing on the kid playing — fair by count, but decide whether Player 2 should face one. **Resolved in #188** (the owner: both face them — plain blows and power moves are counted apart). (g) A guest's pools are asked for under the kid playing, so a co-op battle with a guest makes about twice that kid's question calls (well inside the 20 a minute, and the per-kid budget is spent faster). Fixed in review: Player 2 locked for the fight; no question shared with the hero's pool (`partnerPick`). |
| #186 | 🟢 | Med    | **Built (2026-10-22): 🆚 Quiz Duel — two players on one screen, each at their own level.** A kid asked to play with a sibling or a friend (reverses `DESIGN-JRPG.md` §6 Q4 for one screen). From the Training Grounds: Player 2 is another kid of the family (questions asked for as them — their age, budget and seen questions) or a guest (questions under the kid playing, at their level −2 / 0 / +2); turns alternate, five each; the most right answers wins. See CLAUDE.md → Two players. **Shortcuts, to revisit:** (a) nothing is saved — no XP, levels, streak, play day or Library for either player (no sibling's profile is ever written, which keeps #61's class of bug away); a family kid as Player 2 could earn their own later, with their own profile read and written like the kid playing's. **XP resolved in #188** (both players earn XP; levels and the Library still don't move). (b) A guest's questions are written for the kid playing's age (the server works the age out from the kid's birth date, B8) and count against that kid's question budget; a grown-up guest at 🔥 Harder still gets a kid's questions. (c) One topic for both players; each picking their own would suit siblings who like different things. (d) A family kid joins without their PIN (only their levels are read; their budget and seen questions are used) — ask for it if families want that. **Resolved in #188** (the owner: the PIN is required). (e) Online play stays out of scope. Co-op battles followed as #187. |

| #184 | 🟢 | Low    | **Fixed (2026-10-22, PR #89):** each read of the kid's row is called off after `READ_TIMEOUT_MS` (10 s — an `AbortController` passed with `.abortSignal(…)`, cleared once the read answers; not `AbortSignal.timeout`, which older Safari lacks and fake timers can't drive) and ends as a failed read, so the retry and its back-off carry on; a first read that hangs starts play on the copy, unread, and asks again. The tests' query-builder stubs gained `abortSignal`. TC-2263–TC-2266. **Review (2026-10-22):** the abort reached only the fetch — supabase-js first waits for the session (a token refresh after the tab slept), unbounded — so the whole read now races a timer and ends as a failed read whatever is still pending; and a fixed 10 s could starve a slow link that does answer, so each failed read gives the next twice as long (10 s, 20 s, then `READ_TIMEOUT_MAX_MS` 30 s), back to 10 s on a read, a load or a clear. TC-2286, TC-2287. Nothing left. Was: **A profile read that hangs holds that kid's retries (progress history review round 1, 2026-10-21).** `retryRead` reads one at a time per load (`readingGen`), and supabase-js's fetch has no timeout, so a read that never answers (a captive portal, a half-open connection) holds that kid's timer and change-triggered reads until the browser ends the request (minutes); the next kid is no longer held (fixed in round 1). Possible fix: `.abortSignal(AbortSignal.timeout(…))` on the profile read (the tests' query-builder stubs would need the method). |

Topics saved before this build have no start at all, by design. Now no start is noted until the row has been read (`profileStore.unread`), so a start is lost at worst, never wrong. And `SAVE_VERSION` 4 (an empty step) stops a tab still on a v3 build from loading a save with starts and saving it back without them (it asks for a refresh; the server refuses its lower version). Still open: a start lost with #151's losing copy; topics saved before #29 have none. |

| #168 | 🔴 | Med    | **Changing the login through Supabase Auth can't be held to the 👪 window (found 2026-10-21 building #167 / #124d; reworked in review round 1).** Migration 0024's window guards every table write and RPC, but `supabase.auth.updateUser` (email, password) and `signOut` belong to the auth service, which knows nothing of it: whoever holds the family's sign-in (a kid with the browser's developer tools) can call them directly. **What stands in the way now:** *the email* — "Secure email change" (on by default): the current address must confirm too, so the login can't be moved to another inbox; *the password* — GoTrue asks for an emailed code ("Secure password change", off by default) only from a sign-in **over a day old**, so on its own it never covered today's sign-in on the family device (round 1's critical finding); "require the current password" (`GOTRUE_SECURITY_UPDATE_PASSWORD_REQUIRE_CURRENT_PASSWORD`) covers more — every change needs the current password, except on **any sign-in made through an email link** (reset, magic link, the sign-up confirmation link) for that sign-in's whole life (GoTrue's `IsRecovery()` covers `otp` / `magiclink` / `recovery`; corrected in review round 2), so a family device first signed in through its confirmation link skips it, and there the database's wait is the real defence — and the app always sends `current_password`; and whatever the settings, 0024 notes every password change, and one the app didn't announce means no password opens 👪 or closes the account for 24 hours (the grown-up PIN still opens 👪; a reset link's new password ends the wait; an announcement is for one sign-in and one minute — the device's own sign-in named by a check made after it asked, or the reset link's own sign-in — review round 2), so a kid who changes it can't use it to get in, and the grown-up has a day to take the login back by email ("Forgot password?"). **Owner steps (dashboard → Authentication; names can differ a little by version):** keep "Secure email change" ON (without it, an email moved to a kid's inbox could reset the password and skip the wait); turn ON "require current password" for password updates (if the dashboard doesn't show it, ask Supabase or set it through the Management API's auth config); turn ON "Secure password change" (the emailed code for older sign-ins); turn ON the "password changed" notification email (`GOTRUE_MAILER_NOTIFICATIONS_PASSWORD_CHANGED_ENABLED`); keep `/play/` in Redirect URLs. **Still open:** a full fix routes credential changes through an edge function (admin API, checking the window) with self-service updates off — Supabase has no switch for that, so it stays the owner's call. **Also noted:** a password set in the dashboard, or a hash Supabase re-encrypts, counts as unannounced (the wait starts — the safe side); changing the password cancels an email change still waiting for its links (the done page says so; there's no call to cancel one on purpose — a new change replaces it); Sign out can't be gated (a nuisance, not a risk — signing back in needs the password); "other devices are signed out within about an hour" assumes the default 1-hour JWT expiry (Supabase ends their sessions at once, and 👪 says "Sign in again" there). |

The right grown-up PIN (`try_parent_pin`, and the old `check_parent_pin`) opens it on the sign-in that typed it; a password typed just now opens the device's own sign-in through `unlock_grown_ups(p_session)`, called with the throwaway `passwordCheckClient`'s token (an `amr` entry within 60 s — `password`, or an email link: `otp` / `magiclink` / `email/signup` / `recovery` / `invite`; bare `password`/`pwd` names need `last_sign_in_at` just now; no `amr` refused — and the session named must be one of the login's live `auth.sessions`); a sign-in may open **itself** only while the family is still being set up (no consent, no player, a player without a PIN), so the first run (sign up / sign in → consent → first player → their PIN) needs no gate. The app opens it from the gate, asks again over the page when it shuts mid-action (`RelockDialog`, what was typed kept, the step re-run), shuts it on leaving 👪 Grown-ups or finishing setup (`lock_grown_ups`) and before a grown-up's Sign out. A tab of the old app opened by password gets "couldn't save" for grown-up actions until it reloads (its PIN path still works — `check_parent_pin` opens the window). Tokens must carry `session_id` (Supabase's do; a custom access-token hook must keep it). `docs/PRODUCTION-DB-SETUP.md` still stops at 0021 (its "what's applied" checks don't know 0022–0024 yet). **Before (2026-10-19):** **The 👪 Grown-ups gate is a lock in the UI only (found 2026-10-19 building #124b/c).** Every grown-up action — `set_kid_pin`, `set_parent_pin`, adding / removing kids through RLS, sign-out — needs only the family's session, which a kid on the shared device holds, so a kid with dev tools could skip the grown-up PIN / password gate (or set a new grown-up PIN). Closing an account already re-checks the password server-side (#124b), and PIN guessing is now limited (#124c). **Fix:** a short server-side "grown-up unlocked until" (set by a successful `try_parent_pin` or a password check in an edge function) that the grown-up RPCs and policies require. **Review (2026-10-19, PR #84 round 1):** confirmed not a must-fix for "safe to share" — other families are protected server-side (RLS, `auth.uid()` in every PIN function, `delete_family` service-role only, closing re-checks a fresh password sign-in on the server), and PIN guessing is limited in the database. But schedule it **before a public launch** (the audience is 6–18 since #156, and a teen with dev tools on the family laptop is plausible), and it **must land before #124(d)** (changing the login email / password in 👪 Grown-ups) or anything else credential-bearing goes behind the gate. **Review round 2 (2026-10-20, PR #84):** closing no longer trusts any fresh-password session on the device: `delete-account` takes a token whose `amr` password time is within **60 s** (was 5 min); the close's re-sign-in and the 👪 gate's password check run on a client that keeps nothing (`passwordCheckClient`), so neither leaves such a session in the device's storage or other tabs. **What's left:** for about a minute after a grown-up really signs in on the family device (the sign-in page), the session it stores could still close the account with a hand-made request — a kid with dev tools would need to act inside that minute. Closing it fully needs a second factor the device doesn't hold: Supabase's emailed reauthentication code (`auth.reauthenticate()` + the nonce on the close) or a one-time code from an edge function — an email round trip on the irreversible step; the owner's call. |
| #166 | 🟢 | Low    | **Back to the Library (the owner, 2026-10-20):** now that touch typing (#165) is on `main`, a kid who logs back in should be told Librarian Sage has something for them at the Library, so they go and talk to him about typing. Done: `WorldScreen` says "📚 Librarian Sage has something for you at the Library in Lumina Village — go and see him!" (`LIBRARY_NUDGE`, a world toast, so it's read out) a beat after the world opens, once a session per kid (`battleStore.libraryNudgeSaid`, reset by Switch player and sign-out), until the first typing lesson is cleared (`typingUnlocked`). It waits out a story, an overlay or a toast already up (a retry every 2 s, up to 30 s — past that, next session), counts as said only once it's been up 2.5 s (a battle cutting it short means it's said again next time), plays only once the intro has been seen (a first visit isn't a return) and never on a glass keyboard (no key seen on a coarse pointer — the Library's gate would only turn them back). Decided: every session until lesson 1, not once — the owner's "whenever someone logs back in". Not done: no arrow or 🚩 to the Library (Sage's own lines and the Library card name the lessons once they're there). |
| #165 | 🟡 | Med    | **Touch typing (the owner and their brother, 2026-10-19):** kids don't type any more, so the game teaches it. **(1) Librarian Sage's typing lessons** (`features/typing/`, `content/typing.ts`, `lib/typing.ts`): inside the Library's panel (📚 Library → ⌨️ Typing lessons), 13 lessons in the classic order — the home row from the bump keys out (F J, D K, S L, A ;), the top row, G H, the corners, the bottom row, capitals with the other hand's Shift, then true-fact sentences — each open once the one before is cleared (`typing:<id>` flags), 30 XP the first time, every lesson cleared gives the ✍️ Scribe's Quill badge. Lines are typed against the real keyboard (`useTypingRun`: window keydown/keyup, the keys held tracked) over an on-screen keyboard and two hands (`KeyboardDiagram`): the key to press glows gold, the finger for it lights on the hands. Keys are known by `KeyboardEvent.code` (the physical key, the same on every layout) and matched by `key` (what it typed). **The home-row hold** is the owner's safeguard against one-handed cheating: the other hand must hold its bump key (J for a left-hand letter, F for a right-hand one) — where its pointer belongs anyway — and a capital needs the other hand's Shift, as touch typists really do; nothing for the space bar. Not Shift for letters: Windows opens FilterKeys after Right Shift is held 8 s and Sticky Keys on 5 presses; F/J are plain keys, and two keys down is within every keyboard's rollover. A letter typed without the hold is a nudge ("🖐️ Other hand home! Hold J…"), never a miss, and doesn't count; the right letter in the wrong case says Caps Lock is on; a wrong key is a miss and shivers the line. Accuracy gives 1–3 stars (95% / 85%). The hold is a per-player save setting, `typingAnchor` (additive, like `defendTimer`; on by default), switched in 📜 Menu → ⌨️ Typing and on the lessons list; under 8 the list suggests going slow. **A real keyboard first:** `keyboardStore` remembers the first press from a physical keyboard this session (`watchKeyboard` in `App`; a glass keyboard's keys name no code); the lessons ask for one, with a word for phones and tablets. Typed text never leaves the browser. **(2) ✍️ Scribe bursts in battle** (`features/battle/TypeBurst.tsx`, wired in `BattleArena`): with a real keyboard seen and the first lesson cleared (`typingUnlocked`), a right Attack or block answer is followed, on the question card under the answers (`QuestionCard`'s `afterAnswer`), by a short word to type against a 10 s clock (`BURST_MS`, the defend timer's bar with "to type") — the hero's name, Ember's or the foe's, lowercase (`burstWord`: a long foe gives its last word), so the hold asks for a bump key, never a Shift mid-word. Every right key ticks and sparks ✨ on the hero (each press does something at once — the Undertale feel the owner asked for, without a second input mode). Typed in time, an Attack hits `SCRIBE_ATTACK_MULT` (1.25×, applied when ▶ Go! resolves it through `scribeRef`, so the store stays the truth) and the message says "✍️ Scribe's power — the word was true!"; a block adds `SCRIBE_BLOCK_CHARGE` (1 ◆) the moment the word ends. ▶ Go! early or the clock running out: no bonus, the answer already counted (never a penalty). The hold rule applies as set. **Open:** (g) bursts never follow spells, Pair Attacks or companion strikes (the super-hard question is the whole move) — by design for now; (h) the burst box in the card at 320 px: keyboard-only, so never on a phone, but a small laptop window is untested (TC-1480); (i) a burst's sparks use the hero float — many quick keys queue many floats; fine on the bench, worth a look on a slow machine. **(3) The bonus scroll** (the owner's idea; `features/battle/TypingBonus.tsx`, `rollTypingBonus`): one won battle in three (`TYPING_BONUS_CHANCE`), with a keyboard seen (no lesson needed — it teaches on its own, the keyboard and hands drawn), the victory panel offers a scroll: type the hero's name, Ember's or the foe's, written as it is (a capital wants the other hand's Shift), for 🪙 15 coins, ⭐ 20 XP, a Berry Potion or a Hint Feather. No clock; Onward! without typing is fine. Paid once, at the last key (`claimBonus`: the save or `addXp`). **Open:** (j) the reward table is flat (a quarter each) — a boss's win could offer more, or a rarer item; (k) the scroll uses the victory panel's `max-w-xl` width — keyboard-only, fine on a laptop, untested in a small window (TC-1484). **(4) The name race** (the owner, 2026-10-19: name the critter for first strike, but random, so some critters get the first move; `nameRaceDue`, the `race` turn in `BattleArena`): as a regular critter's battle opens — never a boss, nor a swift critter, which strikes first anyway — with a keyboard seen and lesson 1 cleared, one encounter in three (`NAME_RACE_CHANCE`) opens on a race in the command menu's place: type the critter's name within `NAME_RACE_MS` (6 s; the burst with its own words). Won: the hero strikes first, a free blow at `FIRST_STRIKE_SHARE` (half a landed Attack's power, streak-boosted like any hit), then their own turn (`heroStrike`'s new `then`). Lost: "⚡ … darts in before you can move!" — the critter's turn first, a defend question as for a swift critter. The one typing moment that can cost something, and what it costs is the first move, never HP outright. **Open:** (l) the free blow never triggers Battle Cry's focus or Lionheart (plain `attackDamage`) — by design, it's a nip, not an Attack; (m) a race's result waits 0.7 s so the kid reads it, so a very fast kid sees the blow a beat late. **(5) The scriptorium** (`features/typing/Scriptorium.tsx`, 📚 Library → 📜 The Scriptorium): a scribe's desk where the kid copies out a page line by line over the keyboard and hands — the "here's why" of a question on their Library shelf (learning by writing; the question is named above the line) or a fun fact (`lib/funFacts.ts`, the loading screen's, "Did you know?" dropped). `scriptoriumPages`: up to `SCRIPTORIUM_SHELF_PAGES` (3) shelf pages first, then one fact a topic round the ten topics, `SCRIPTORIUM_PAGES` (6) at a time; a page copied before (`page:<id>` flag) leaves the desk, so it refills. `scribeText` makes a passage typeable (curly quotes and dashes to their plain keys, an ellipsis to three dots) and drops any with a character off the key map (an accent, a degree sign — every fun fact passes, a test says so); `pageLines` wraps at `PAGE_LINE_MAX` (40) with words whole. Pays `SCRIPTORIUM_XP` (15) once a page — a starter question's page too: the XP is for the typing, a page copies once, so nothing to farm (the #153 rule is about knowing answers). Opens once the capitals lesson is cleared (`scriptoriumOpen`, `SCRIPTORIUM_OPENS_AFTER`): every page has big letters and full stops; before that the Library's button says so. **Open:** (n) the facts are finite (50): a keen scribe copies them all and the desk then refills only from the shelf — more pages could come from the question bank's explanations (a saved-only request, screened) later; (o) a shelf page is keyed by the question's id, so beating the question (it leaves the shelf) takes its page with it, copied or not — by design; (p) digits and symbols on a page ("5,050", "99.8%") have no lesson, only the key map and the hold — fine to copy, worth a number-row lesson later. **(6) The song that breaks the hush** (`BOSS_SONGS` / `songFor` in `content/typing.ts`, the `song` turn in `BattleArena`): the Hush Fiend (the Crystal of Voices, Act III) winds up its Great Shush every third turn like any boss; with a keyboard and lesson 1, that wind-up is a typing moment instead of the plain warning — a line of the first Sage's lullaby (four lines, lowercase letters and spaces, one per wind-up round and round) against `SONG_MS` (20 s). Sung in time, the move fizzles: its next blow is a plain one (`setIntent('attack')` — the one exception to `nextIntent`'s "a charge is always followed by its power blow"); too slow, the usual warning and the ×2 blow as ever, and Guard or a Rainbow Ward still block it, so the song is never a penalty. Each key floats 🎵 on the hero. Only the Hush Fiend sings for now; any boss gets a song by adding it to `BOSS_SONGS`. **Open:** (q) the song's four lines are mine — the owner may want the first Sage's real words (STORY-4X) once they're written; (r) a boss with a song and a swift critter's first move were not tested together (no such boss); (s) the wind-up banner ("gathering power for Great Shush") still shows above the song box for 2.8 s — right, since the move is coming unless the song breaks it, but busy on a small window (TC-1491). **(7) 🍂 Falling Leaves** (`lib/leaves.ts`, `features/typing/FallingLeaves.tsx`, 📚 Library → 🍂 Falling Leaves, from lesson 1): letters drift down on leaves (`LEAVES_TUNING`: a 7 s fall and a leaf every 1.8 s at first, quicker the more are caught, floors at 3.5 s / 0.9 s; five in the air at most); the right key catches the lowest such leaf, a key with no leaf is a wrong key (a nudge, never a penalty), the hold applies as set (a leaf wants the other hand's bump key, as in a lesson); three leaves on the ground, or the 60 s clock, end the round. Only the plain characters of keys the lessons cleared so far have taught fall (`leafPool`: f and j after lesson 1; the pool stops at the first lesson not cleared), so the game never asks for a key the kid hasn't learned. The keyboard under the field points at the lowest leaf. Accuracy first: the result is leaves caught and keys right; XP only for milestones, once each — 10, 25 and 50 in one round (`LEAF_MILESTONES`, `leaves:<n>` flags, `LEAVES_XP` 20) — so nothing to farm. **Open:** (t) no reduced-motion mode: the game is the motion; a slower "calm wind" setting could come with a grown-up switch; (u) the leaves fall at the same pace at every age — the defend timer scales by age, this could too; (v) a round pays nothing past 50 — a leaderboard or a daily best would need a number in the save (flags hold none). **(8) Pangram chests** (`PANGRAMS`, `pangramChest`, `pangramFor` in `content/typing.ts`; the scribe's lock in `PathQuestionOverlay`): about one riddle-chest in four (`PANGRAM_CHEST_EVERY`, by a hash of the chest's id, so a given chest always has its lock, with the same sentence) also has a scribe's lock: with a keyboard and the capitals lesson, a box above the riddle offers its pangram — a sentence with every letter of the alphabet, six kid-safe ones, each checked for all 26 letters and the key map — typed line-run style (the keyboard and hands under it); done, the chest opens with its usual coins (and key item) plus `PANGRAM_BONUS_COINS` (15), no XP (no question was answered), and the riddle goes. The riddle stays on offer as ever, so no chest is ever locked to a kid without a keyboard. **Open:** (w) the lock's box sits above the question on a laptop — a tall riddle plus the lock needs the overlay's scroll (it scrolls) and is untested in an 800×600 window (TC-1496); (x) a chest opened by its lock pays no XP where the riddle's right answer pays `XP_PER_CORRECT` — the coin bonus stands in, by design; (y) the same six sentences repeat across the world's ~15 locked chests; more pangrams are one line each. **(9) Pen-pal replies** (`PEN_PALS` / `penPalFor` / `penPalReply` / `penPalReadLine` in `content/typing.ts`; `DialogueOverlay`'s writing panel; `QuestConversation.questId` / `step`): Shush's Pen-Pal Papers (#75 item 15f) gets a line in the kid's own hand. At the bring step — Librarian Sage taking Shush's letter — with a keyboard and the capitals lesson, the last line offers ✍️ Add a line of my own (never focused: Enter reads on as ever): pick one of three lines, type it over the keyboard and hands, and the step finishes as it would (the letter handed over) with the line remembered by its number (`penpal:<quest>:<n>` — never free text, so nothing a kid types reaches the save or the server); back at Shush, the quest's ending reads the line out before the reward. Maybe not goes back to the letter; the step still finishes by ✨ Got it! or 📚 Library as before. Any letter quest gets one by adding to `PEN_PALS`. **Open:** (z) the three lines are mine; (aa) a kid who hands the letter over before clearing capitals never sees the offer (the step finishes as ever) — by design, no second visit; (bb) the writing panel is a second `ModalLayer` in the same overlay (focus moves to it); untested in an 800×600 window (TC-1498). **Review round 1 (2026-10-19; a fresh code reviewer, a fresh UX reviewer over 53 screenshots of the real app at 1024×768, 800×600 and 1280×800 from the new `bench … typing` mode; the owner: fix all):** code — 0 critical/high, 3 medium, 5 low, all fixed: the scribe's lock stayed up after the riddle had opened the chest and paid its bonus on an open chest (now gone once solved; the bonus rides a real opening only); the name race won against a shielded critter still gave the critter the first move (`heroStrike`'s shield-broken branch ignored `then`); the bursts, race and song ran clocks for a player whose defend countdown is off (now: no race; bursts and the song untimed, "No clock — take your time", the song box offers "Let it come — I'll Guard it"); Falling Leaves read a bare F or J as a wrong-hand nudge when it was the other hand going home for the lowest leaf, and kept a key "held" into the next round; the caps nudge said Caps Lock when a Shift was held by habit (`capsLock` from the event tells them apart); the keyboard store was written on every key; tests that couldn't fail. UX — 0 critical, 4 high, 9 medium, 9 low, all fixed but two kept: Space on a focused button was swallowed while any line was up (now the button's, unless the line wants a space next — `onControl`); a screen reader heard neither the line nor the next key nor a result (sr-only text in the line, the next key in the status line between nudges — `hints.ts` `keyHint` — results announced and their first button focused); the song and race cards ran off the bottom at 1024×768 and the bonus scroll drew the full hands under the spoils (cards scroll into view, one heading each, the scroll's keyboard compact); no way out of the 20 s song (the let-it-come button in both branches); words split mid-word in a wrapped line (`TypingLine` wraps by word); the keyboard jumped on a nudge (the status line keeps its height); the scribe's lock buried the riddle (an offer first, "✍️ Type the sentence" unfolds it; the heading says the lock sprang); `animate-pulse` ran under reduced motion and a held anchor differed from an unheld one by motion alone (`motion-safe:`, a rim on a held anchor); the next lesson sat below twelve ✅ rows (a ▶ Continue button up top, focused; locked rows `aria-disabled`, in the Tab order); the leaf that counts wasn't marked (a white ring; punctuation drawn big); the space bar sat bottom-left (rows centred); the Caps Lock nudge lit no key (the Caps key flashes); the race on F and J alone read as a punishment (it waits for lesson 11, every letter's, and gives little hands 9 s; one heading); finger names ("pointer", "little finger"); the pen-pal panel scrolls in a small window and sets no flag until the line is written; plainer words ("Time's up", "every letter and dot", "the round ends"); the Menu's 11 px line. Kept: "Leave" under "Stop for now" mid-lesson (every service panel's exit); no Esc on these overlays (as the app). Logged: the block burst stacks its bar under the stopped defend timer; `(pointer: coarse)` wording on a touchscreen laptop. **Review round 2 (2026-10-19; fresh code and UX reviewers over the round-1 screenshots; the owner: fix all):** code — 0 critical/high, 2 medium, 5 low, 2 nits, all fixed: `enemyWord` cut a hyphenated one-word name mid-word ("Cuttle-Scribe" → "cuttle-scrib"; now a hyphen is a space and a long name is never cut — a test sweeps every `ENEMY_DEFS` name); after a wrong riddle answer the pangram overlay focused "Hmm… I'll come back!", so a double or early Space (let through to a focused button since round 1) clicked it and closed the chest mid-sentence (`focusContinue={!lockShown}`; the run also takes Space's keyup, since Firefox clicks on keyup); Falling Leaves wrote `addXp` once per milestone back to back (one write for the round's milestones); it had no caps handling (a capital of a falling letter is a `caps` nudge, a bare Space nothing); `watchKeyboard` wrote the store on every key; "Let it come" stayed live during the 700 ms beat after the line was sung (out of reach once settled); `PANGRAMS[1]` ended in "!", a key no lesson teaches (a full stop now; a test keeps every pangram to taught keys); a run typed over any focused text field (`inField` exempts them); `LITTLE_HANDS_AGE` lived in a component module (now `content/typing.ts`). UX — 0 critical, 2 high, 7 medium, 3 low, all fixed: the bursts and the song ran clocks from lesson 1, on F and J alone (now no clock until every letter's lesson, `lettersLearned` / `typingClock`); focus fell to the page when a burst settled and when a lesson line replaced its Start button (▶ Go! focused as the burst settles, `QuestionCard`'s `focusContinueKey`; the line's box takes focus); a per-line remount never announced the new line or the result (one live region per lesson and per desk page); the next-key hint read a whole sentence a key (`keyName`: "J", "comma"); the race was red from its first second and started before hands left the arrows (urgency a third of a short clock; a 1.2 s "Ready…" beat, `RACE_READY_MS`; 8 s / 12 s for little hands; the song 30 s under 8); the Library's locked desk and game looked like the open lessons card (🔒, `aria-disabled`, "Clear typing lesson 12 (capitals) first"; a glass keyboard is told "Needs a real keyboard"); the hold switch sat far below the tip naming it; "Stop for now" read as a pause ("← Back to the lessons", "⏹ Stop the round"); the keyboard vanished when a burst was done, so ▶ Go! jumped (it stays, dimmed); the bursts didn't say what the word does ("Type it for a harder hit!"); a held Enter could skip the one-time pen-pal offer (nothing focused there); the song's lyric had a lone "i" and a long prompt; "Why: Why does ice float?"; confetti under reduced motion; the lock's open line repeated the heading; the leaves field too tall in a short window. **Review round 3 (2026-10-19; fresh code and UX reviewers over 55 fresh screenshots; the owner: fix all):** code — 0 critical, 1 high, 1 medium, 4 low, 3 nits, all fixed: a burst, race or song whose clock ran out showed a green "✓ In time!" (round 2's `readyMs` work passed `stopped` for the late state too; the timer now has a "⏰ Time's up" state and `TypeBurst` stops it only on a typed word); with the lock unfolded, a riddle answered right left focus on the page (`focusContinue` now waits only while the lock is actually up); the race's "Ready…" beat was drawn as the Forget-Me-Knot's violet "⏸ Paused" with its aria (`DefendTimer` `ready`: a plain "Ready…", "Get ready — N seconds to type"); an untimed burst never settled, so ▶ Go! was never focused (the burst takes focus on mount); Falling Leaves' round left focus on the page (the field takes it); round 2's third-of-the-clock urgency had shortened the ordinary 10–14 s defend clocks for kids 13+ (the third applies below 10 s only — `features/battle/urgency.ts`, unit-tested); a key down as a run ended stayed drawn "held"; "Typed in time" for a word; the orphan comment and dead alias from the `LITTLE_HANDS_AGE` move. UX — 0 critical/high, 4 medium, 4 low, 7 lows and 2 nits, all fixed but one left to the owner: a typing moment outside the Library never told a screen reader what to type (the burst, the bonus scroll, the chest's lock and the letter read "Type: …" before the first key); a finished lesson or desk line vanished on its last key, so a kid typing in rhythm pressed into the next line (it stays `LINE_HOLD_MS` = 400 ms all green with a brighter tick; keys in the beat do nothing); the pen-pal offer could still be Enter-ed past by a kid who had advanced with Enter (the carry-on button is a new node beside the offer, so nothing is focused); the chest's heading rode up into the HUD band with the lock unfolded (padding, and a compact lock); "Today's letters" said the set changes daily ("Your letters so far: … — more with each lesson"); "every leaf badge is yours" promised a badge that doesn't exist; a word that ran out mid-type handed the next Space to ▶ Go! (the focus waits 600 ms on that path); leaves tilted up to 40° (15° now); the desk shows the whole passage, the line being copied bright; the nudge line is two lines tall on a phone; the song's label is shorter ("💢 Great Shush — sing to break it!"); the leaves count is read with the lowest leaf; drill lines are spelled to a screen reader ("F F F, J J J", `spellLine`); the leaves result says why the round ended. Tests +13 (TC-1504, TC-1505); 1694 green, lint + tsc clean; `bench … typing` rerun. **Review round 4 (2026-10-19; the owner asked for two more rounds; fresh code and UX reviewers over round 3's 55 screenshots; fix all):** code — 0 critical/high, 1 medium, 4 low, 2 nits, all fixed: the desk's line-hold timer survived "← Back to the pages" and could move a page opened in the beat past its last line (a crash in render; cleared on Back and on opening a page, with a test); a word typed in time handed ▶ Go! the focus on the last key, so a trailing Space or Enter resolved the turn (a 300 ms beat on that path too); `spellLine` put a comma pause between every word of the sentence lessons (words read as a sentence; a word's trailing mark is named, "fall semicolon"); `QuestionCard` re-scrolled Continue when `focusContinue` turned, pushing the lock's line off-screen (scroll and focus are separate effects); a live region born with "Type: …" may not be read (filled a tick after mount); three sounds on a lesson's last key (the run ticks on `typed` only; the card's chime waits for its beat); two tautological row checks in `TypingLessons.test`. UX — 0 critical, 1 high, 3 medium, 8 low, 1 nit, all fixed: a mark up on a key ("!", "?", "(", "%") lit the plain key, and pressing it was a counted miss with "Not that one. Look at the glowing key!" — now the right key with the wrong Shift is a `caps` nudge, never a miss, the nudge names the key ("! is up on the 1 key: hold Shift with your other hand"), the lit key shows the mark and the Shifts light for it, `keyHint` says "Press Shift and the 1 key (!)", and the pen-pal lines lost their "!"; the pen-pal line never read "Type: …" to a screen reader (`announce`); the last key of a lesson, a page, the pangram or the letter swapped to a result with a focused button at once, so a habitual Enter closed it unread (the same `LINE_HOLD_MS` beat on every last line; the flag, coins and XP pay at once); after a wrong riddle answer the lock's copy still said "answer the riddle as ever" ("The riddle got you — but a scribe can still open it…"); the capitals lesson's intro told the kid to hold F or J ("…and for a capital, hold that hand's Shift instead"); the leaves intro never mentioned the clock; little hands got the same 10 s burst as everyone (`BURST_LITTLE_MS` 15 s); the line was read three times (one group label now; the big size steps up from `sm` so a twelve-letter word fits a phone); the chest's heading sat in the HUD band with the lock up (`pt-24`); the desk's hands were cut at 800×600 (compact under 700 px tall); the pen-pal typing state had no direct way out ("Maybe not" beside "Pick another line"). Tests +5 (TC-1506, TC-1507); 1699 green, lint + tsc clean; `bench … typing` rerun. **Review round 5 (2026-10-19; the last of the five; fresh code and UX reviewers over round 4's 55 screenshots; fix all):** code — 0 critical/high, 1 medium, 4 low, 2 nits, all fixed: with the hold on, round 4 lit the typing hand's Shift amber for a shifted mark — the very Shift the hold rule rejects (now the other hand's Shift only, and none when a Shift anchor is already blue; a `KeyboardDiagram` test); the "right key, wrong Shift" nudge misfired on a non-QWERTY layout (it now needs the key to have typed one of the lit key's two characters); a digit pressed for "!" with Caps Lock on flashed the Caps key (Caps Lock is blamed for a letter only); "Pick another line" / "Maybe not" stayed live in the letter's beat and the riddle stayed answerable in the lock's (both inert for the beat); the race's and the song's last key went silent for 700 ms (the burst ticks it); a capital with the hold off had no Shift cue (the other hand's Shift lights, "Press the right Shift and the E key"); and both reviewers on the line's label: a label on an otherwise empty group reads once on some readers and not at all on others, so the line is hidden text again (one reading, navigable) and the bench reads `data-line`. UX — 0 critical/high, 1 medium (the same Shift), 6 low, 2 nits, all fixed: a replayed lesson's card says "You cleared this lesson before — no XP this time"; the bonus scroll's keyboard stays dimmed once the name is typed, so Onward! doesn't jump; the clock's tick was the same blip as a typed key (a typing clock keeps to its red pulse); the letter goes compact in a short window, as the desk does (`shortWindow`); the lesson intro names the gold key and the blue key; the focused boxes have names ("Line 2 of 5", "Your line", the burst's prompt); the leaves clock reads "⏳ 55s"; a drill line is spoken spelled on the line itself too (`TypingLine` `spoken`). The live task now reads "Type this: …" (the line's own hidden text is "Type: …"). Tests +4 (TC-1508, TC-1509); 1703 green, lint + tsc clean; `bench … typing` rerun. Left as noted by round 5: `shortWindow` is read at render (no resize listener); a 320 px phone shot of a twelve-letter burst word would settle the round-4 "fits a phone" change (the bench runs at three larger sizes); an accidental Tab mid-line lands on "← Back to the lessons". **Review round 6 (2026-10-20; the owner asked for two more rounds; fresh code and UX reviewers over round 5's 55 screenshots; fix all):** code — 0 critical/high, 1 medium, 4 low, 2 nits, all fixed: an anchor key held through a line's beat (or down before a run mounted) was unknown to the new run, so the first key of the next line nudged "Hold J" while J was held — `keyboardStore` now keeps the keys physically down (`keysDown`, capture-phase listeners from `watchKeyboard`), a run and a leaves round start from them, and a repeat of a key never seen going down counts as a press; a capital typed with Shift under Caps Lock said "hold Shift" while the Caps key flashed (Caps Lock is blamed first, when known); under the hold the keyboard's hint had lost round 4's "(!)" (named either way); the burst's round-5 last-key tick doubled the scribe's `streak` (the tick is the race's and the song's, in the arena); the riddle card under the lock's beat was `aria-hidden` yet focusable (`inert` now); the lesson's live line omitted the badge; an untimed burst read its label twice. UX — 0 critical/high, 2 medium, 6 low, 2 nits, all fixed: three result moments arrived as fresh text with no live region — the leaves' settled card, the lock springing open and the sealed letter (each component keeps one live region across its states now); on Sage's offer line the gold 📚 Library button quietly spent the one-time pen-pal line (it no longer finishes the step there, and is the quieter button); the leaves round box had no name; a trailing Space in a burst's beat scrolled the battle page; "➡️ Next page" opened the top of the desk, not the page that followed; the race and song cards had a thicker white band on top (the burst's margin is the slot's); a reader skipped "the ; key" and the leaves' letter list ("the semicolon key (;)", the marks named); the lesson's announcement omitted the badge, the desk's the no-XP line, the scroll's what was claimed; "Time's up" said twice ("No bonus this time — the answer still counts."); the race's late text repeated the message that followed ("Too slow!"); the burst's status row is two lines tall on a phone; "Press the space bar with a thumb". Tests +4 (TC-1510, TC-1511); 1707 green, lint + tsc clean; `bench … typing` rerun. Noted, not changed: the race is the one typing moment with no way out (the song has "Let it come") — an owner's call; a replayed lesson's intro could say up front that it pays nothing; the lessons list refocuses "Continue" on every return. **Review round 7 (2026-10-20; the second of the two; fresh code and UX reviewers over round 6's 55 screenshots; fix all):** code — 0 critical/high/medium, 5 low, 4 nits, all fixed: a run read the keys down at render, not as its listeners attached (a key let go in between stayed held — read in the effect now); `keyboardStore` remembered a chord's key (macOS sends no keyup for a key pressed under ⌘ — a chord is never held; a hidden tab lets go of everything); the leaves' letter list named the marks in an `aria-label` on a span, which some readers skip (hidden text now); the leaves' result was read before the milestone tally was in, so a milestone a beat later read it twice (`tallied`); the riddle's `inert` was spread as a prop, which React 19 reads as a boolean (set as an attribute, as `useInertOutside` does); the burst's clock read its label a second time; the bonus scroll's claimed line repeated the reward the status row says; `nudgeText`'s `capsLock` semantics and the desk's `paid` guard explained. UX — 0 critical/high, 1 medium, 6 low, 2 nits, all fixed: the Library's sub-screens left a keyboard at the top of the page — the desk's list, the keyboard gate, the lessons list once every lesson is cleared (no Continue to land on) and the shelf on the way back (the button that led away is gone) — each heading takes the focus now (`tabIndex={-1}`, no ring); "Page copied!" and "Lesson cleared!" were skipped by a reader, the focus landing on Next page / Next lesson past the stars and the XP (the result's heading takes it); "A scribe's reward — well typed!"; a long desk title lost its end (`line-clamp-2`); the leaves' wrong-key nudge said nothing for a mark ("No leaf says ";" (semicolon)"); a lesson's title is `text-base` on a phone; the leaves' copy promised a finger check the game doesn't make ("Press each one before its leaf lands"); the lock's spoken opening carried a trailing 🪙; the riddle's confetti respects reduced motion. Tests +4 (TC-1512, TC-1513); 1711 green, lint + tsc clean; `bench … typing` rerun. Noted, not changed: a page's title with "!" or "%" is spelled by the reader's own rules (content); the 320 px phone shot is still unverified; the race's no-exit remains an owner's call. **Open / decided:** (0) left to the owner from round 3: with the home-row hold on at lesson 1 the leaf pool is f and j only, so every leaf is a two-hand chord with the holding hand swapping each time — the hardest round is the first; accept a bare F or J while the pool is only f and j, or keep it as the hold's first practice? Also: should the Hush Fiend's song wait for every letter's lesson like the race (today it runs from lesson 1 with no clock and "Let it come")?  (a) the drawn labels are QWERTY's — on AZERTY or Dvorak the finger map stays right (physical codes) but the letters drawn may not; Chromium's `navigator.keyboard.getLayoutMap()` could label them later. (b) The hold proves the HAND, never the finger — nothing but a camera could, and a camera has no place in a kids' app; the design says so. (c) Open to every age (no grown-up switch); the little-hands note and the hold switch stand in — the owner may want a per-kid switch in 👪 Grown-ups later. (d) Speed is not scored, on purpose: accuracy first, words per minute later and gently. (e) The 📜 Menu's ⌨️ Typing switch has no component test (the lessons list's does). (f) The Library's panel is `max-w-xl`: at 320 px the keyboard's letters are ~5 px — readable as a map of where to press, not as labels; a wider, landscape-only layout for the lessons could come later. **Numbering:** this entry was #159 on its branch (its commits say so); `main` took #159 for the issues audit, so it is #165 since the merge (2026-10-20). |
| #164 | 🔴 | Low    | **Tailwind's `min-[…]` / `max-[…]` variants make no CSS here (found 2026-10-19, #159).** The `touch` / `touch-wide` entries in `tailwind.config.js` → `screens` are objects (`raw` media queries), and object screens turn off Tailwind's `min-*` / `max-*` variants (`max-[359px]:`, `min-[360px]:`, `max-sm:` …) — they compile to nothing, silently. The battle top bar's `max-[359px]:hidden` and `StreakBadge`'s `min-[360px]:inline` had been dead since #130 B2; both now use raw `[@media(...)]` (#158b) and CLAUDE.md → Conventions says so. **Real fix:** register `touch` / `touch-wide` with a plugin (`addVariant('touch', '@media (pointer: coarse)')`) instead of `screens`, so the normal variants work again — check that `touch:` classes keep winning over `sm:` ones (variant order) before switching. |

tests added for the per-kid routing, the catch path's carried questions and the "+N XP" on a mixed round (all 14 of the reviewer's mutations and 19 of this round's fail a test); the mid-round wait's clock starts once the old question has slid out (`leaving`, `onExitComplete`), so a question that lands meanwhile never flashes "One moment…" nor waits out its minimum; (a) Fixed: `ageToStartLevel` was `age − 5`, so a new player of 15+ started every topic at level 10, the hardest *for their age*; it now stops at 6. **To revisit:** (b) Questions saved before ages were stored (`age` null) still reach a 6-year-old and were written for 7–11. | #153 | 🟢 | High   | **Closed (2026-10-19, deployed):** the owner applied `apply_all_migrations.sql` (0001–0021) and redeployed `generate-questions` after #159 merged — look-aheads use saved questions only. (d) stays a watch note: grow the starter bank if a long outage makes six a band repeat. **Audit (2026-10-19, #159):** closes with the `generate-questions` redeploy (the same one as #152), so look-aheads use saved questions only. (d) is a watch note. **Starter questions, and zone look-aheads that cost nothing (Act I launch review B5, the app half).** (a) **A child never sees a question error:** `fetchQuestions` (`lib/questions.ts`) never fails and never comes back short — whatever the server can't give (offline, a 503, today's budget spent (429), a topic the deployed function doesn't know yet, a short batch) comes from a hand-written bank, `content/starterQuestions.ts`: 10 topics × 3 bands (levels 1–3, 4–6, 7–10) × 6 questions, the right answer first and shuffled as served, each with a short explanation; a batch bigger than a band (a battle's 9) tops up from the nearest band. A failure is logged (`console.warn`). Every starter passes `screenQuestion` (starterQuestions.test). Ids start `starter-`: `markSeen` and `flagQuestion` skip them (`inQuestionBank`). (b) **Zone look-aheads are saved-questions-only:** entering a zone warms every living critter's battle (`WorldScreen`), and each warm-up used to be a full request that could write fresh questions with Claude for battles that mostly never happen. They now send `cacheOnly: true` (`prefetchQuestions(…, { cacheOnly })`): the function sets `freshAllowance = 0`, takes only unseen saved questions (never the kid's seen ones), and answers 200 even when it has none. When the battle starts, `fetchQuestions` uses the warmed batch and asks the function for only the questions still missing (normally, so fresh ones can be written), then starters. The next-quiz-round look-ahead (`QuizRound`) stays a normal request (saved-only too since #163). An old function ignores the flag (a full batch, as before), so either deploy order works. (c) **Kept:** the "Coming soon!" screen (`TOPIC_NOT_READY`) can no longer show — a topic the function doesn't know gets starter questions instead; the copy and its test stay for now. (d) **Open:** six questions a band repeat quickly for a child on starters for a long time (a day with the service down) — grow the bank if that happens; a quiz round made of starter questions still moves the skill ramp and pays XP like any round (they're real questions at about the right level); starter questions show 🚩 Report, which does nothing for them. (e) **Review round 1 (2026-10-16, `/saas-code-review`: 1 medium, 3 low), all fixed:** starter questions no longer move the saved skill level — six a band repeat (one band covers three levels), so a child on starters (a topic the deployed function doesn't know, an outage) could learn them by heart and climb +2 a flawless round to level 10, then meet level-10 questions when the service came back; now a quiz round's ramp counts bank questions only, a battle's ramp too (`rampAnswers`), and a starter answer never feeds the speed trigger — XP still pays (the conservative call; (d)'s ramp item is resolved); no 🚩 on a question that isn't in the bank (starter or `fresh-`) — it said "Reported — thanks! We'll take a look" and sent nothing ((d)'s 🚩 item is resolved); five starter questions fixed — continents no longer offer 5 (the count taught in some countries), the solar panel no longer offers "Heat only" (a solar hot-water panel does that), the bridge question asks for the shape that can't be pushed out of shape (arch bridges are curves), "highest" not "tallest" mountain (Mauna Kea), the first Olympics "about" (not "nearly") 2,800 years ago; tests now cover a warm-up that fails while the battle waits for it and a question handed back twice (the PR's "warming failed" test used an empty batch). (f) **Review round 2 (2026-10-16, `/saas-code-review`: 1 medium, 1 low), both fixed:** round 1's bank-only ramp let one or two bank questions decide a round topped up with starters (a short batch: a thin bank with the budget spent, or Claude down) — a round passed 4 of 5 dropped a level on its one bank answer, a round failed 1 of 5 climbed two, a battle won on one right bank answer among starter answers climbed two; now `bankRamp` (`lib/age.ts`) ramps on the bank answers only while they're more than half the answers given, else the level holds (the conservative call), in `QuizRound` and `BattleArena` (`answerCount`); and a speed boost no longer swaps the battle's bank questions for starters with "The questions just got harder" (its "nothing came back" path was dead since `fetchQuestions` never fails) — the harder pool keeps bank questions only, and with none the battle keeps its pool and says the level goes up after it. Tests: age.test, QuizRound.test (the mixed-round test now fails without round 1's filter), BattleArena.test. (g) **Decided (owner, 2026-10-16): starter questions pay no XP.** A right starter answer earns none anywhere — the quiz, battles (the win's own XP still pays), gates, chests and riddles (they still open), shrine trials (they still teach the spell), the Spire (its first-clear bonus still pays; a re-clear on starters says nothing about XP) and the Library (a starter miss beaten again leaves the shelf with "🌟 Beaten!", no XP). Replaces round 1's "XP still pays". (h) **Merged with B9 (#152):** a look-ahead holds nothing on the family budget (`p_fresh_wanted: 0`, TC-1401). |

on real numbers a burst of 8 kids × 20 calls × 15 questions against 400) and the family lock serialized nothing that mattered: `begin_question_request` now takes `p_fresh_wanted` (the batch size) and holds the allowance on the request row until the function writes what it made (always now, 0 when Claude fails; an unexpected error leaves the hold — never an unlimited bill; a sibling's call that lands mid-hold may get fewer fresh questions for that batch, served from the cache); a removed kid's rows were kept for good against the notice's "deletes their information straight away" — the function deletes them once a day old (a partial index; `delete` granted to the service role) and the draft notice says a count is kept, without the name, for a day; the backfill had no test (TC-1327 said it had) — the test re-applies 0021 over an old row. 0021 also drops the five-argument version its first draft made. ⚠️ A project that applied the first draft of 0021 must re-run `apply_all_migrations.sql` (`db push` skips a recorded version). **Review round 2 (2026-10-16, code: 1 medium, 2 low), all fixed:** removing a kid left every request row they'd made (unlinked, each with its time) until the next question request anywhere deleted the ones over a day old — hours in a quiet spell, and that request then deleted the whole history under its family's lock — so a `before delete` trigger on `profiles` (`profiles_forget_requests`, security definer) now deletes at once their rows over a day old or with nothing spent, keeping only today's spend; an error between the quota check and the usage write kept the whole hold for the day — the catch-all now writes what Claude made before it (and, without 0021, no longer leaves that spend at 0); TEST-CASES' "I" type is in the legend. **Open after round 2:** (e) a removed kid's spend from today goes at the first question request after it's a day old (no scheduled job), so in a quiet project it can outlast the notice's "one day". |
| #151 | 🟡 | High   | **Deployed (2026-10-19):** the owner applied `apply_all_migrations.sql` (0001–0021) and redeployed `generate-questions` after #159 merged. 0020 is in. Left: decide on (a) a per-push compare-and-swap; run (e) TC-1325 on real devices. **Audit (2026-10-19, #159):** left: decide on (a) a per-push compare-and-swap, run (e) TC-1325 on real devices, and apply 0020 before shipping. **Saves kept the wrong copy (launch checklist S1).** (1) When the server had a save it always won on load, so progress kept only on the device (made while the server couldn't be reached, or in the 2 s before a tab closed) was overwritten. (2) Nothing saved when a tab closed (the server write is on a 2 s debounce). (3) With two devices or tabs the last save won, silently. **Done (2026-10-16):** every save has a `revision`, one more per change (additive: a save without one counts as 0 — no `SAVE_VERSION` bump, since old apps are stopped by the server rule below and an old tab that loads a revisioned save can only lose its own unsaved moves); loading keeps the higher revision of this device's copy and the server's (a tie, or neither with a revision, goes to the server's, as before) and pushes a newer local copy; migration **0020** extends 0011's trigger: a stored revision may only be replaced by a higher one or by the identical save (a retried upload), never by a save without one (an old app tab → its existing "refresh" screen); a row without a revision takes anything until the new app saves it once, so nobody on the old app is blocked before they refresh; same error `save_version_conflict`, with detail `save_revision_conflict`. A refused tab shows "🧭 Your adventure moved on!" and marks its local copy revision 0 so the refresh loads the server's; a tab also stops when another tab on this browser saves newer progress (`storage`) or the server is ahead when it comes back into view; a hidden or closing tab pushes at once with a `keepalive` fetch; `flush` no longer resends a copy the server already has. **Open:** (a) between two copies that *both* moved on, **the one with more changes wins** — a counter, not a merge: the other tab or device is refused and told to reload, but a stale copy that made more changes before its first push still wins. Sending the revision a copy was based on (a per-push compare-and-swap) would make that strict; not done here. (b) The first load after this ships can't tell which pre-revision copy is newer and keeps the server's, as before. (c) A save on a closing page is best effort: the keepalive request needs a CORS preflight to Supabase, Firefox only honours `keepalive` since 133, and a save over 60 KiB or an expired token falls back to a normal request that may be cut off — the local copy always has it and wins the next load on that device (another device sees it only after that). (d) Editing a save by hand in SQL now needs its revision raised too (0020's header shows how). (e) TC-1325 (real devices and browsers) not run yet. **⚠️ Deploy:** apply `0020_save_revisions.sql` (or re-run `apply_all_migrations.sql`) **before** shipping this app build — it changes nothing for today's app (no save has a revision yet), and the new build relies on it to refuse older copies. **Review round 1 (2026-10-16):** one finding, fixed: the save on hide sent the token `authStore` held even when it had expired (certain 401 — (c) said it fell back, the code only did with no session), and a hide push that failed was never retried while the page stayed open, since hiding had cancelled the debounce. Now a token expired or within 30 s of it goes the normal way (supabase-js refreshes it), and a failed hide push (not a refusal) re-arms the 2 s debounce (TC-1361–1362). Trigger, load / flush races and listeners checked, nothing else found; (a) stays open. **Review round 2 (2026-10-16):** no bug found in the code — every load / flush / hide / refusal path, the retry, the listeners, the copy and old app tabs against a revisioned row hold up. Two test gaps, closed: five data-loss guards in `saveStore` could each be deleted with the suite green (the "only our own copy" check before marking it revision 0, the load checks on a late push answer and on a late "back in view" check after a sign-out and the next kid, the back-forward-cache check, the retry being for hide pushes only), and the SQL test never checked that a version refusal lacks the `save_revision_conflict` detail the app picks its screen by, nor a JSON-null or a stored junk revision (TC-1363–1368; each new test fails with its guard removed). Noted for (a): the hero's position is saved every 1.5 s of walking, so walking counts as changes. |

| #147 | 🟡 | High   | **Deployed (2026-10-19):** the owner applied `apply_all_migrations.sql` (0001–0021) and redeployed `generate-questions` after #159 merged. Left (owner, CLAUDE.md → Commands): add yourself to `public.admins`, set the alert secrets, schedule the pg_cron digest, run the bank recheck (dry run, then `--apply`); then read ~50 questions per topic at ages 7, 9 and 11 and tune effort / timeout from the logs. **Safer questions (2026-10-16): new prompts, Haiku 5.5, an explanation check, reuse by age, a bank recheck.** A rewrite of the question prompts, then a `/saas-code-review` of it (2 high, 2 medium, 3 low), all fixed on the owner's say-so, plus the owner's call to move from Claude Haiku 4.5 to **Claude Haiku 5.5**. **Done:** (1) **the model:** `MODEL = 'claude-haiku-5-5'` in the shared screening block (the function and the recheck both read it). Haiku 5.5 thinks by default and thinking counts toward `max_tokens`: the writer gets 16,000 (was 4,096) and the checks 8,000 / 4,000 — the blind check's old 1,024 would have been cut off and silently dropped every fresh question to the cache. Effort per call (writer and blind check `medium`, explanation check `low`), adaptive thinking, timeout 30 s (worst case 120 s, under the function's 150 s). New with Haiku 5.5: refusals (`stop_reason: refusal`, no server-side fallback) — `replyJson` throws with the category, so the batch comes from the cache. (2) **prompts:** ten numbered safety rules held to every part of a question — wrong options and the explanation too — adding privacy (no personal questions), nothing a child could copy, stereotypes, conquest and slavery, disputed borders, disputed or changing facts, facts true in one country only; the writer pitches the level to the child's age with examples at 7, 9 and 11, levels 9–10 may reach two years ahead in ideas, and it checks its own work (thinking is on, so it can); the blind check fails a question two options could answer and ignores instructions inside the questions. (3) **the explanation check:** the blind check never sees the explanation (it would give the answer away), so it had only the word filter — a second call now reads each fresh question with its right answer and explanation (`EXPLAIN_PROMPT`, `keepExplained`), side by side with the blind check; both must pass, and each drop is logged with the reason (`problem` in both schemas). (4) **pointing at a place:** `screenQuestion` drops "all / none of the above", "both A and B", lettered options and "the answer is B" in any part — the options are shuffled twice; a letter that is an option's own text passes (music notes, the alphabet). (5) **topics:** history drops conquest and slavery, geography disputed borders, territories, capitals and country counts. (6) **reuse by age (migration 0017):** the picker kept out questions written for older kids only, so with levels pitched to age an 11-year-old at level 10 got mostly 7- and 8-year-olds' questions; now within `AGE_SLACK` (1 year) either way, and an older child's question only at or below the kid's level. The new parameters default to null (0016's behaviour), and a function deployed before 0017 retries the picker without them (logged), so deploy order is free. (7) **the bank recheck:** `supabase/scripts/recheck-questions.ts` (Deno, the owner's service-role key; dry run by default, `--apply` to act) runs every active, not-yet-reviewed saved question through the screen and both checks at the age it was written for (`--default-age`, 9, for rows without one) and moves failures to `review` with `questions.review_note` (0017) — only if still in the status it read; the review queue now lists held-back questions with no reports, the note among `reasons`. (8) **caching:** the three prompts are the same for every child (the age moved to the user message) and carry `cache_control`; Haiku 4.5's 4,096-token minimum meant the old marker did nothing (#134a), Haiku 5.5's is 512. Each call logs its time and tokens (`logCall`). (9) **checked once, and again on a report (the owner's follow-up):** migration 0018 adds `checked_version` / `checked_at` (new questions are saved with `RULES_VERSION` when the checks ran; the recheck skips checked questions and marks what passes) and `report_checked_at`; after a report the app asks the function (`action: 'check-report'`), which claims the question (`claim_report_check`: only a report by this family since the last check, ≤ 20 a family a day) and runs `reportReview` — Haiku, then **Claude Opus 5.5** if Haiku finds nothing (higher effort, the server-side refusal fallback); a problem or a check that can't run holds it back, a check never puts one back; `--reported` sweeps reports no check covered. (10) **alerts and the admin page (the owner's follow-up):** a report check that leaves a question held back alerts the owner at once, and a daily digest (`action: 'digest'`, pg_cron, a secret header) lists everything waiting — by email (Resend), Slack and/or Discord, whichever secrets are set (`_shared/notify.ts`); migration 0019 adds `public.admins`, `is_admin`, `admin_review_queue` and `admin_review_question` (records `reviewed_by`), and 👪 Grown-ups gets 🛡️ Question review (Release / Approve / Retire) for an admin; the alert's `?review` link opens it after the Grown-ups gate. This closes #133(b) (a daily digest). **⚠️ Deploy (owner):** apply 0017, 0018 and 0019 (or re-run `apply_all_migrations.sql`); add yourself to `public.admins`; set the alert secrets (`RESEND_API_KEY`, `ALERT_EMAIL_TO`, `APP_URL`, `ALERT_DIGEST_SECRET`; Slack / Discord optional); `supabase functions deploy generate-questions`; schedule the digest (CLAUDE.md → Commands); run the recheck as a dry run, read what it would hold, then `--apply` (and `--reported --apply` now and then). **Open:** (a) **not run against the real Claude API or Supabase** — checked with a local harness (stand-in Supabase + Claude, 52 checks) and Postgres 16; before relying on it, generate ~50 questions per topic at ages 7, 9 and 11 and read them, and watch the function log's drop reasons and "cached" token counts for a day (TC-1278); (b) effort levels and the 30 s timeout are first guesses — tune from `logCall`'s times; (c) the writer and both checks are the same model, so blind spots can line up — a stronger model for the checks is a one-time cost per question (each is checked once, then reused); (d) the age window makes pools thinner for ages few kids share, so more fresh questions (cheaper now); (e) a recheck of the whole bank costs two calls per 15 questions — `--limit` and `--topic` let it run in parts; it skips `approved` questions and anything already reviewed unless told otherwise; (f) the review queue's `reasons` mixes report reasons with the recheck note (`recheck: …`), to keep 0016's columns so the bundle stays re-runnable; (g) a report check that passes leaves the question as the report rules left it (one "Not OK for kids" still hides it until the owner looks) — the owner's to change if the AI should restore it; (h) a report made in a transaction that started before a claim and committed after it could be missed by `report_checked_at` (a narrow race); `--reported` won't see it either — the queue still lists it; (i) an Opus check is roughly a few cents a report — the daily cap bounds a family's spend; (j) email goes out from Resend's test sender (`onboarding@resend.dev`) until a domain is verified, and that sender only delivers to the Resend account's own address — set `ALERT_EMAIL_FROM` after verifying one; (k) the digest schedule (pg_cron + pg_net) stores the anon key and the digest secret in `cron.job` (visible to database admins only) — Supabase Vault would hide them, if wanted; (l) a question reported again while already held alerts again (one alert per report that starts a check); (m) Release / Approve / Retire can't be undone from the page (Retire asks first) — `select review_question(id, 'active')` in the SQL editor puts any question back; (n) not run against real Resend / Slack / Discord or the deployed app (TC-1295). |
